- **principle-bounded-repair**: Bound every repair loop — retrying an identical failure the same way burns budget without generating information. Set a repair budget before fixing, change the hypothesis or the observation when a retry fails the same way twice, and escalate to a blocker report when the budget expires instead of weakening acceptance. Apply during any fix loop, CI-chasing, or repeated verification failure. ([vraj-ai/skills](https://github.com/vraj-ai/skills/tree/HEAD/principles/principle-bounded-repair) · ★3)

- **principle-bug-fixing**: バグを修正するときに、症状を抑えるパッチではなく根本の原因を直すためのスキル。原因を特定し、同じ原因を持つ箇所を洗い出し、再現テストで原因を捉えてから、原因を取り除く最小の差分で直す。 コードの不具合の修正を求められたときは、依頼がテストや原因に触れていなくても必ずこのスキルを使うこと。 「バグ直して」「不具合を修正して」「〜になってしまう、修正お願い」「エラーが出るので直して」「問い合わせがあった件を直して」といった依頼で使う。原因の調査だけを頼まれた場合にも使う（コードやテストは変更せず、原因と同じ原因を持つ箇所を報告する）。 ([kazukit-dev/dotfiles](https://github.com/kazukit-dev/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/agents/skills/principle-bug-fixing))

Bulkification is necessary but not sufficient for large data volumes. ([krika2810/sfstack](https://github.com/krika2810/sfstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-bulkify-by-default) · ★1)
- **principle-calibrated-uncertainty**: Apply whenever Jev returns probabilities or confidence, and whenever writing projections, diagnoses, or predictions. Act on high confidence, confirm the middle band, clarify or escalate the low band; never fake certainty. ([frinfo702/cowork](https://github.com/frinfo702/cowork/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-calibrated-uncertainty))
- **principle-caller-obligations**: Simplify API use by moving repeatable mechanics to their owner and comparing language builtins; use for caller setup, ordering, lifecycle, or error duties. ([leesangb/agents](https://github.com/leesangb/agents/tree/HEAD/plugins/sb/skills/principle-caller-obligations) · ★1)
- **principle-caption-states-the-conclusion**: Apply when writing a figure caption for a paper or manuscript. State the finding first, then what the elements are, then n, the test and what the error bars mean. ([taixuann/rstack](https://github.com/taixuann/rstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-caption-states-the-conclusion) · ★1)
- **principle-capture-the-baseline**: WHEN about to change something whose result you will verify or compare; NOT for read-only investigation or syncing a git base; records pre-change evidence so the check reads old value versus new value. ([mintuz/skills](https://github.com/mintuz/skills/tree/HEAD/src/principle/skills/principle-capture-the-baseline) · ★37)
- **principle-central-provider-wiring**: Apply when every feature Provider or call site must pass the same cross-cutting prop (logger, inspector, telemetry, error boundary, auth): wire it once at a root Provider, with per-site overrides. ([cevr/dotfiles](https://github.com/cevr/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-central-provider-wiring) · ★1)
- **principle-challenge-from-raw-data**: Apply when the task treats a belief as given. Reproduce that belief from raw material. ([jl-cmd-projects/DeepStack](https://github.com/jl-cmd-projects/DeepStack/tree/HEAD/plugins/deepstack/skills/principle-challenge-from-raw-data))
- **principle-challenge-shared-premise**: Question the premise shared by repeatedly failing fixes and choose an observation that can disprove it before another attempt. ([leesangb/agents](https://github.com/leesangb/agents/tree/HEAD/plugins/sb/skills/principle-challenge-shared-premise) · ★1)
- **principle-challenge-the-brief**: Apply when a founder states who their customer is, what works, or why deals happen. Check claims against evidence instead of writing them down. ([ycanerden/gluely](https://github.com/ycanerden/gluely/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-challenge-the-brief) · ★3)
- **principle-change-sync**: Repeatable workflow for synchronizing APT principle updates across doctrine, checklists, prompts, and references. Use when a principle changes and linked enforcement artifacts must be updated consistently. Triggers: principle update, terminology change, checklist drift, prompt drift, reference contract alignment. ([adthomps/apt-principles-agents](https://github.com/adthomps/apt-principles-agents/tree/HEAD/platforms/github-copilot/source/skills/principle-change-sync))
- **principle-chase-y-not-x**: Apply when a request arrives already shaped as a solution (X): find the real need (Y) behind it before building, and propose a better X when one serves Y. ([cevr/dotfiles](https://github.com/cevr/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-chase-y-not-x) · ★1)
- **principle-cheapest-layer-that-catches-it**: Apply when you choose where a test lives. Put each check at the lowest layer where you can observe the failure: static, unit, component or contract, API or integration, end-to-end, then manual or exploratory. Do not repeat one assertion across layers. ([Jacky97s/qstack](https://github.com/Jacky97s/qstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-cheapest-layer-that-catches-it) · ★1)
- **principle-check**: Grade a proposed feature, scope change, or design decision against felis's four design principles before any code or doc work begins. Returns a per-principle verdict with the exact failing Test clause for any violation. ([felis-terminal/felis](https://github.com/felis-terminal/felis/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-check) · ★9)
- **principle-check-the-facts**: Use before writing assumptions, a spec, a design or a rollout plan: never build on a business fact nobody confirmed. Lists the business facts a decision rests on and sources each from CLAUDE.md, .fbs/context.md or the user; asks the missing or stale ones. ([fabricio21x/fbs](https://github.com/fabricio21x/fbs/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-check-the-facts) · ★1)
- **principle-checklist**: Apply engineering principles as evidence-producing checks at the moment each is cheap — while planning a change (plan), while fixing each review finding (fix), and before commit/PR (finish) — or build a project's own principle list from its PR/MR review history (harvest). Use when starting a non-trivial change, when addressing review feedback, before declaring a change ready, or when a team wants to turn past review comments into checks. ([yuzu-tsuki/claude-skills](https://github.com/yuzu-tsuki/claude-skills/tree/HEAD/principle-checklist))
- **principle-ci-cd-and-automation**: Sets up CI pipelines and their quality gates. Use when creating or modifying a CI pipeline, adding automated checks or test runners to CI, adding preview deploys, or speeding up a slow pipeline. ([joseiciano/dotfiles](https://github.com/joseiciano/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/ai-clis/global-ai/output/claude/skills/principle-ci-cd-and-automation) · 2 copies)
- **principle-cite-or-delete**: Apply when writing any claim in a spec, issue, report, or verdict. Every claim carries its source citation, confidence label, and media where applicable. A claim you cannot cite is deleted, not kept with a hedge. ([jirayusueb/qa-stack](https://github.com/jirayusueb/qa-stack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-cite-or-delete))
- **principle-claims-rest-on-records**: Apply before stating any number, status, or citation to the supervisor, when judging a subagent's or tool's report, and when a result looks too good. ([tosunlab/labstack](https://github.com/tosunlab/labstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-claims-rest-on-records) · ★1)
- **principle-classify-before-you-fix**: Apply when a test fails. Name the failure class with evidence before you change anything: product regression, test defect, contract or doc drift, environment, data collision, flaky, or known issue. Do not edit a test or expected value to match the observed behavior unless the class is test defect and the oracle source agrees. ([Jacky97s/qstack](https://github.com/Jacky97s/qstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-classify-before-you-fix) · ★1)
- **principle-clean-architecture**: Clean Architecture, hexagonal architecture, ports and adapters, and dependency rule. Auto-load when designing architecture, choosing layers, ports, adapters, or keeping the domain free of framework code. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-clean-architecture) · ★3)
- **principle-clean-architecture-dotnet**: The layered architecture for a .NET system of this class — Domain / Application / Infrastructure with thin composition-root hosts (API, Worker, UI). Defines dependency direction, what each layer may reference, the patterns this standard deliberately omits, and how SOLID is applied. Use when placing a new type, adding a project reference, or deciding where a concern belongs. ([oskarc/AssetHub](https://github.com/oskarc/AssetHub/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/principle-clean-architecture-dotnet) · ★2 · 2 copies)
- **principle-clean-code**: Clean code, DRY, KISS, YAGNI, function length, abstraction level, function argument count, Boy Scout Rule, intent-revealing naming. Auto-load when writing functions, reviewing code clarity, or debating whether to abstract. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-clean-code) · ★3)
- **principle-clean-up-what-you-started**: WHEN tearing down after a run, verification or failed attempt; NOT for making reruns converge or reverting unrelated edits; releases only resources this run created and keeps the evidence. ([mintuz/skills](https://github.com/mintuz/skills/tree/HEAD/src/principle/skills/principle-clean-up-what-you-started) · ★37)
- **principle-clear-naming**: Apply when naming variables, functions, types, files, or endpoints. Enforces the 6-month amnesia test, language conventions, intent-based naming, and cross-boundary consistency. ([Scino/fstack](https://github.com/Scino/fstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-clear-naming) · ★13 · 2 copies)
- **principle-close-or-block-the-dimension**: Apply at the end of any planning session. Every dimension ends either settled or as a named decision ticket - never merely discussed. ([mattlindell/photon-plugins](https://github.com/mattlindell/photon-plugins/tree/HEAD/plugins/ph-plan/skills/principles/principle-close-or-block-the-dimension))
- **principle-code-comment**: コードのコメントに何を書き、何を書かないかを決めるスキル。コメントには原則として Why not（素直な書き方や代案を採らなかった理由）だけを書き、How はコード、What はテスト、Why はコミットメッセージに任せる。 コードを書く・編集するとき、コメントを書く・直す・消すとき、コメントや JSDoc をレビューするとき、「コメント足して」「コメントいる？」「このコメントどう？」と言われたときは必ずこのスキルを使うこと。テストコードのコメントにも使う。 ([kazukit-dev/dotfiles](https://github.com/kazukit-dev/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/agents/skills/principle-code-comment))
- **principle-code-first-documentation**: Treat generated OpenAPI and its documentation UI as part of every HTTP boundary. Mandatory when creating, changing, reviewing, or diagnosing an HTTP server, route, handler, request or response schema, or API error contract. ([ElmoJones3/opinionated](https://github.com/ElmoJones3/opinionated/tree/HEAD/skills/engineering/principle-code-first-documentation) · ★1)
- **principle-code-quality**: Load before writing or changing code in any language, and before reviewing a diff, designing types or interfaces, refactoring, or debugging. Covers cross-language limits, naming, code smells, type and boundary discipline, domain modelling, reader load, deletion-first sequencing, and scope rules, plus on-demand references for Python, TypeScript, C#, GDScript, and Godot. ([Uraxii/dotai](https://github.com/Uraxii/dotai/tree/HEAD/plugins/pstack/skills/principle-code-quality))
- **principle-code-review**: Code review heuristics — four-axis review lens (correctness, security, design integrity, test coverage); confidence-based filtering (no finding without a concrete failure scenario); review comment tone (observation over accusation); nitpick filtering; what counts as a real finding vs linter noise. Auto-load when writing or framing a review comment, deciding whether a PR finding is worth surfacing, reviewing a diff for correctness, or filtering review nitpicks. ([aibot88/sec_skill_store](https://github.com/aibot88/sec_skill_store/tree/HEAD/skills/claudskills/principle-code-review) · ★6)
- **principle-code-review**: Code review heuristics — five-axis review lens (correctness, security, design integrity, test coverage, comment quality); review comment tone (observation over accusation); nitpick filtering; distinguishing a real finding from linter noise. Auto-load when writing or framing a review comment, reviewing a diff for correctness, or filtering review nitpicks. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-code-review) · ★3)
- **principle-code-simplification**: Simplifies working code without changing behavior. Use when code is harder to read or maintain than it should be, or to deslop a branch of AI-generated code patterns. For prose, use unslop. ([joseiciano/dotfiles](https://github.com/joseiciano/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/ai-clis/global-ai/output/claude/skills/principle-code-simplification) · 2 copies)
- **principle-code-speaks-for-itself**: Apply whenever you write or edit code. Never add a comment that explains the code or your work on it. Say it with names, types, smaller functions, and tests; your work goes in the commit message and the review. ([daryl-tg/deez-skills](https://github.com/daryl-tg/deez-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-code-speaks-for-itself))
- **principle-codebase-design**: Shared vocabulary for designing deep modules. Use when the user wants to design or improve a module's interface, find deepening opportunities, decide where a seam goes, make code more testable or AI-navigable, or when another skill needs the deep-module vocabulary. ([joseiciano/dotfiles](https://github.com/joseiciano/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/ai-clis/global-ai/output/claude/skills/principle-codebase-design) · 2 copies)
- **principle-coding**: 設計・コード・テスト・変更の進め方で判断に迷ったときに参照する、名前で通じる原則の一覧と、原則どうしがぶつかったときの優先順位。コードやテストを書くとき、レビューするときに使う。 ([sasamuku/dotfiles](https://github.com/sasamuku/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-coding) · ★10)
- **principle-color-means-one-thing**: Apply when choosing colours, markers or a colormap for a figure. A condition keeps one colour in every figure, and the figure still reads in greyscale and for colour-blind readers. ([taixuann/rstack](https://github.com/taixuann/rstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-color-means-one-thing) · ★1)
- **principle-column**: Write a short opinion column that names a trend the reader has already noticed, grants it, flips it in one short sentence, defines a principle in two plain sentences, concedes that many reject it, and closes on an assertion plus one hedge. Use this skill for product-management and leadership columns, newsletter opinion pieces, "my take on X" posts, principle explainers, and any 600 to 1,700 word piece for executives and practitioners deciding how to organise; also when the user asks for "a column", "a short opinion piece", "a hot take that still holds up", or "explain the principle behind X".… ([3orionkings/writing-skills](https://github.com/3orionkings/writing-skills/tree/HEAD/plugins/writing-skills/skills/principle-column) · ★2)
- **principle-comments-as-design**: Use comments as a design tool to capture abstractions, intent, and decisions code can't express. Use when designing a module, interface, or data structure whose intent won't be obvious from the code alone, and when the user asks to "add comments", "document this", or "explain this code". ([andrezzoid/newsroom](https://github.com/andrezzoid/newsroom/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-comments-as-design))
- **principle-communication**: Caveman output mode — ultra-terse brief replies with fewer tokens. Strips filler, hedging, pleasantries. Three levels — caveman lite (default), caveman full, ultra caveman / max caveman (maximum compression). Persists until normal mode. Auto-load when the user requests terser output, says "be brief", "less tokens", "caveman mode", "use fewer tokens", or invokes `/caveman`. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-communication) · ★3)
- **principle-completion-evidence**: Before claiming completion, directly verify the final artifact or requested behavior and show the evidence supporting that claim. ([leesangb/agents](https://github.com/leesangb/agents/tree/HEAD/plugins/sb/skills/principle-completion-evidence) · ★1)
- **principle-compose-vue-components**: Apply when building or refactoring Vue components. Design accessibility first, organize by responsibility, and express layout variants through composition. ([alexanderop/pstack](https://github.com/alexanderop/pstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-compose-vue-components) · ★1)
- **principle-composition-and-mechanisms**: Keep feature policy at its composition point and extract mechanisms only for independent obligations; use for assembly and interface boundaries. ([leesangb/agents](https://github.com/leesangb/agents/tree/HEAD/plugins/sb/skills/principle-composition-and-mechanisms) · ★1)
- **principle-composition-over-flags**: Apply when a component, function or API grows boolean props or mode flags: split it into primitives that compose instead. ([cevr/dotfiles](https://github.com/cevr/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-composition-over-flags) · ★1)
- **principle-concise**: Apply to any prose you produce: replies, PR descriptions, commit messages, docs, agent-facing instructions. Short declarative sentences, no filler, no bloat; every sentence earns its place by changing a decision. ([RestartDK/dotfiles](https://github.com/RestartDK/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/config/agents/skills/dstack/principle-concise) · ★1)
- **principle-concurrency**: Concurrency principles — race conditions, livelock, backpressure, lock-free primitives, choosing between mutex vs channel vs actor vs semaphore vs CAS. Auto-load when designing concurrent code, fixing a deadlock, choosing a synchronization primitive, or reasoning about goroutine/thread/task lifetimes. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-concurrency) · ★3)
- **principle-confidence-from-many-perspectives**: Apply when you are about to call a design, plan, ticket or root-cause claim done, or a change that would be costly if wrong. ([kapetan-io/dispatch](https://github.com/kapetan-io/dispatch/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-confidence-from-many-perspectives))
- **principle-conflict**: Run CLAUDE.md's Principle Conflict Protocol when applying an Engineering Principle would conflict with an existing rule, established pattern, or current implementation — including a violation discovered mid-task. Stops work, explains the conflict, and offers three resolution paths for the developer to choose. Use the moment a conflict is noticed, not after picking a side. ([Bluecode77732/Upload-Board-Project](https://github.com/Bluecode77732/Upload-Board-Project/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/principle-conflict) · ★1)
- **principle-constraint-driven-development**: Constraints — a project's quality bar written down in CONSTRAINTS.md with numbers and commands, and guarded against agents lowering it. Use when setting up quality standards or gates (coverage, security, accessibility, performance), when you need a threshold and have no number, or when an agent silences checks, skips tests, or edits thresholds down to get green. ([joseiciano/dotfiles](https://github.com/joseiciano/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/ai-clis/global-ai/output/claude/skills/principle-constraint-driven-development) · 2 copies)
- **principle-constraint-moves**: Apply after relief work ships, a checkpoint fires, or metrics shift. Re-identify the binding constraint. Yesterday's bottleneck is not permanently sacred. ([Shixuuu/grokbot-pi-templates](https://github.com/Shixuuu/grokbot-pi-templates/tree/HEAD/templates/community/research-briefings/first-principles-t5qkap/skills/principle-constraint-moves) · ★6)
- **principle-contain-untrusted-work**: Treat lower-trust data as data and restrict the authority and resources of code, parsers, plugins, tools, and workers that process it. Mandatory when lower-trust input is interpreted or executed, selects a path, URL, query, or command, or receives access to files, networks, secrets, tenants, or sensitive effects. ([ElmoJones3/opinionated](https://github.com/ElmoJones3/opinionated/tree/HEAD/skills/systems/principle-contain-untrusted-work) · ★1)
- **principle-context-boundaries**: Prefer local authoritative project evidence and retrieve external information only to resolve concrete factual gaps. ([leehosanganson/dotfiles](https://github.com/leehosanganson/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/ai/.config/ai/skills/principle-context-boundaries))
- **principle-contextualize-and-write-for-the-reader**: Contextualize and write for the reader. - **principle-coordinate-external-effects**: Move durable intent across transaction and ownership boundaries through explicit delivery, reconciliation, and compensation protocols. ([ElmoJones3/opinionated](https://github.com/ElmoJones3/opinionated/tree/HEAD/skills/systems/principle-coordinate-external-effects) · ★1)
- **principle-corrections-become-tests**: Apply whenever a human corrects a label, draft, score or claim. ([ycanerden/gluely](https://github.com/ycanerden/gluely/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-corrections-become-tests) · ★3)
- **principle-correctness-and-failure-paths**: Trace correctness and failure paths; preserve error context and recovery ownership when logic, guards, errors, or fallbacks change. ([leesangb/agents](https://github.com/leesangb/agents/tree/HEAD/plugins/sb/skills/principle-correctness-and-failure-paths) · ★1)
- **principle-correctness-before-speed**: Apply before you time, report, or keep any kernel change. A perf number counts only after an allclose check and a NaN count pass on the same run. ([anhminhnguyenhoang/mstack](https://github.com/anhminhnguyenhoang/mstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-correctness-before-speed) · ★1)
- **principle-correctness-over-pragmatism**: Apply when tempted by a shortcut (a cast, a stub, a guard that masks a crash, a downgraded plan): take the structurally correct fix unless the owner explicitly asked for speed. - **principle-cost-aware-delegation**: Apply when delegating to subagents, CI or paid APIs: budget turns and scope before the handoff, front-load context, cap scope, and commit passing work before the budget runs out. - **principle-cost-awareness**: Cost awareness principles — FinOps mindset, right-sizing, cost-per-request model, storage tier selection, cardinality explosion, log volume. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-cost-awareness) · ★3)
- **principle-creation**: Extract reusable decision-making principles from experiences - especially failures - to build a personal or organizational repository of codified learning. ([sethmblack/paks-skills](https://github.com/sethmblack/paks-skills/tree/HEAD/principle-creation) · ★10)
- **principle-customer-data-stays-home**: Apply before any list of people or companies goes to an outside tool, such as an enrichment tool, a scraper or a sender. ([ycanerden/gluely](https://github.com/ycanerden/gluely/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-customer-data-stays-home) · ★3)
- **principle-data-modeling**: Data modeling — relational vs document vs KV vs graph selection, normalization depth, indexing strategy, hot-key avoidance, schema evolution via expand–contract, query-first design, retention and archival. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-data-modeling) · ★3)

- **principle-ddd**: Domain-Driven Design (DDD) — bounded contexts, entities, domain events, and repository pattern. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-ddd) · ★3)

- **principle-decision-trail**: Record consequential choices with evidence during long-running, unattended, or multi-session work; preserve pivots without inventing history. ([leesangb/agents](https://github.com/leesangb/agents/tree/HEAD/plugins/sb/skills/principle-decision-trail) · ★1)
- **principle-decision-trail**: Apply to long, autonomous, or high-stakes work, and whenever a JEV decision changed the course. Keep a reviewable record of decisions, confidences, and sources. - **principle-deep-module-design**: Design modules with simple interfaces and rich implementations. Use when creating, extending, or refactoring any module, class, service or API. - **principle-deepdive-html-style**: Generate a single-file deep-dive HTML article in the "principle-deepdive" visual style — dark/light dual-theme, indigo/amber accent palette, inline-SVG hand-drawn diagrams, collapsible cards, citation panels, sticky glass nav, zero external dependencies, offline-readable under 300KB. - **principle-defensive-history**: Recover the incident or contract behind unclear defensive code before removing it; missing rationale is not proof it is unnecessary. ([leesangb/agents](https://github.com/leesangb/agents/tree/HEAD/plugins/sb/skills/principle-defensive-history) · ★1)
- **principle-define-done**: WHEN starting a multi-step deliverable, phase or plan, or deciding whether one is finished; NOT for single-step answers or choosing which checks to run; sets a checkable completion criterion first and reports every unmet gate. ([mintuz/skills](https://github.com/mintuz/skills/tree/HEAD/src/principle/skills/principle-define-done) · ★37)
- **principle-define-errors-away**: Remove error conditions by redesigning the API instead of handling them. Use when designing an API's error surface, when error handling in a module is getting dense, or when the user asks to "simplify error handling" or "reduce exceptions". ([andrezzoid/newsroom](https://github.com/andrezzoid/newsroom/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-define-errors-away))
- **principle-define-the-domain-first**: Use when a new noun, state, or stored field appears, one word is doing two jobs, or two sources use different words for what may be one thing. ([ShravanSunder/sunstack](https://github.com/ShravanSunder/sunstack/tree/HEAD/plugins/sunstack-dev/skills/principle-define-the-domain-first))
- **principle-define-the-question**: Apply at the start of an analysis, before writing the first query. Write down the decision the analysis serves, the metric definition, the population, the time window, and the result that would change the decision. Without this, analysis drifts into fishing. ([hidenobunagai/astack](https://github.com/hidenobunagai/astack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-define-the-question) · ★1)
- **principle-delegate**: How to spawn and trust subagents. Use before delegating work, and when a subagent's report comes back. ([gmdkaio/kstack](https://github.com/gmdkaio/kstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-delegate) · ★1)
- **principle-delegate-after-the-contract**: Assess delegation when work could use separate contexts, parallel execution, or a less capable model. ([BumpyClock/agent-templates](https://github.com/BumpyClock/agent-templates/tree/HEAD/skills/pstack/principle-delegate-after-the-contract) · ★2)
- **principle-delegate-implementation-review-stays-here**: Apply when handing work to a subagent. Implementation delegates; design, review, verification, and git mutations stay with the lead. ([daryl-tg/deez-skills](https://github.com/daryl-tg/deez-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-delegate-implementation-review-stays-here))
- **principle-deliver-and-prove**: Establish readiness to deliver an implementation and verify its expected behavior before editing. Use when beginning implementation or when new findings change what delivery or proof requires. ([ahmadabdalla/aasystem](https://github.com/ahmadabdalla/aasystem/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-deliver-and-prove) · ★1)
- **principle-depend-on-caller-contracts**: Apply when you design a dependency or interface, or call through a collaborator to reach another object. ([nilpath/nilpath-marketplace](https://github.com/nilpath/nilpath-marketplace/tree/HEAD/dist/claude/plugins/nstack/skills/principle-depend-on-caller-contracts) · 3 copies)
- **principle-dependencies**: Dependencies that point one way, onto the language's own tools and stable public APIs, one home for each library, the runtimes a project targets, floating ranges with a pinned lockfile, and deciding logic kept pure. ([Nusnus/nustack](https://github.com/Nusnus/nustack/tree/HEAD/plugin/skills/principle-dependencies))
- **principle-derive-dont-sync**: Apply when about to store a value that could be computed from existing state, or to keep two copies of one truth in step: derive from one source instead. ([cevr/dotfiles](https://github.com/cevr/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-derive-dont-sync) · ★1)
- **principle-derive-from-one-owner**: WHEN the same fact or decision is computed or stored in more than one place; NOT for modelling legal states or collapsing pass-through layers; gives it one owner that other sites derive from or receive. ([mintuz/skills](https://github.com/mintuz/skills/tree/HEAD/src/principle/skills/principle-derive-from-one-owner) · ★37)
- **principle-design**: A design built from proven patterns and SOLID objects that put behaviour with the data it serves, short because it fits the problem. ([Nusnus/nustack](https://github.com/Nusnus/nustack/tree/HEAD/plugin/skills/principle-design))
- **principle-design-for-concurrency**: Use when actors already overlap: two workers, retries, callbacks, background tasks, or late results can touch the same resource. ([ShravanSunder/sunstack](https://github.com/ShravanSunder/sunstack/tree/HEAD/plugins/sunstack-dev/skills/principle-design-for-concurrency))
- **principle-design-for-failure**: Use when a call, job, or state change has an effect someone else can see and it can time out, be retried, partly succeed, or end without you knowing. ([ShravanSunder/sunstack](https://github.com/ShravanSunder/sunstack/tree/HEAD/plugins/sunstack-dev/skills/principle-design-for-failure))
- **principle-design-for-operations**: Keeps software correct and identical wherever it runs: crash-only design (fails, stops halfway, runs twice, runs concurrently), idempotent jobs, append-only data runs, and twelve-factor config, logs and releases. Use when writing jobs, pipelines, ETL, workers, CLIs, retries, config, env vars, secrets, logging, deployment or releases. ([Med-Karim-Ben-Boubaker/karim-skills](https://github.com/Med-Karim-Ben-Boubaker/karim-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-design-for-operations) · ★1)
- **principle-design-foundations**: Resolve competing design criteria from the current use case, trusted values, ownership, and concrete costs rather than a fixed architecture. ([leesangb/agents](https://github.com/leesangb/agents/tree/HEAD/plugins/sb/skills/principle-design-foundations) · ★1)
- **principle-design-harness**: AI エージェントに UI を作らせても品質がぶれないよう、制約・文脈・検証・フィードバックの 4 層で作業環境を整えるための原則。こぎそ氏が提唱した「デザインハーネス」に基づく。 ([sasamuku/dotfiles](https://github.com/sasamuku/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-design-harness) · ★10)
- **principle-design-patterns**: Design patterns — Gang of Four and beyond. Strategy, factory method, observer, decorator, adapter, facade, template method, command, repository, dependency injection. Auto-load when designing class structure, refactoring toward a pattern, or evaluating whether a pattern applies. ([aibot88/sec_skill_store](https://github.com/aibot88/sec_skill_store/tree/HEAD/skills/claudskills/principle-design-patterns) · ★6)
- **principle-design-patterns**: Design patterns — Gang of Four and beyond. Strategy, factory method, observer, adapter, template method, dependency injection. Auto-load when designing class structure, or evaluating whether a pattern applies. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-design-patterns) · ★3)
- **principle-design-system**: デザインシステムを作る、直す、使うときに、それぞれの要素がどうあるべきかを判断するための原則。基本原則、部品とパターン、トークン、運用の観点。Alla Kholmatova『Design Systems』をはじめ、広く参照される文献に基づく。 ([sasamuku/dotfiles](https://github.com/sasamuku/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-design-system) · ★10)
- **principle-design-system-first**: Apply to any UI work. Start from the app's existing design system, tokens, and primitives; a new primitive needs justification. Sweat transitions, alignment, and error states; every feature earns its place. ([RestartDK/dotfiles](https://github.com/RestartDK/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/config/agents/skills/dstack/principle-design-system-first) · ★1)
- **principle-design-the-core**: Encodes rules in the shape of the data and keeps them in a pure core: sum types, state machines, tables, value objects, parsing at the edge, I/O in thin adapters, ports only where something really varies. Use when choosing types, schemas or data structures, placing new logic, or wiring a database, API, CLI, queue or LLM client. ([Med-Karim-Ben-Boubaker/karim-skills](https://github.com/Med-Karim-Ben-Boubaker/karim-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-design-the-core) · ★1)
- **principle-design-to-the-port-not-the-vendor**: Apply when writing or changing a port's contract, adding a product or a delivery mechanism behind one, naming a config table or glossary term after a product, or writing code that branches on which product or source is in use. ([kapetan-io/dispatch](https://github.com/kapetan-io/dispatch/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-design-to-the-port-not-the-vendor))
- **principle-deterministic-tests**: Apply to any test that uses time, randomness, ordering, network, concurrency, or asynchronous UI. The same code and the same inputs must give the same result. Wait on conditions. Do not use fixed sleeps. ([Jacky97s/qstack](https://github.com/Jacky97s/qstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-deterministic-tests) · ★1)
- **principle-deterministic-tests**: Tests that each fail when, and only when, the behaviour they guard breaks. ([Nusnus/nustack](https://github.com/Nusnus/nustack/tree/HEAD/plugin/skills/principle-deterministic-tests))
- **principle-discomfort-is-a-signal**: Apply when the named constraint feels easy or flattering, or when the owner tours opportunities and activity lists. Prefer the uncomfortable answer. Activity lists are the default dodge. ([Shixuuu/grokbot-pi-templates](https://github.com/Shixuuu/grokbot-pi-templates/tree/HEAD/templates/community/research-briefings/first-principles-t5qkap/skills/principle-discomfort-is-a-signal) · ★6)
- **principle-distributed-systems**: Distributed systems principles — CAP, consistency models (linearizable, causal, eventual, read-your-writes), quorum, leader election, partitioning, idempotency across nodes, fallacies of distributed computing. Auto-load when reasoning about CAP/PACELC trade-offs, choosing a consistency model, ordering events with logical clocks, distinguishing exactly-once delivery from exactly-once effects, designing replication or partitioning strategy, or assessing distributed failure modes. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-distributed-systems) · ★3)
- **principle-divide-the-work**: Divide the work when a task splits into independent parts, such as several files, sources, or checks whose results do not depend on each other, or when one step would flood the main thread with bulk output. Give each part to its own subagent and merge short summaries. ([ChandlerWeeks/chandler-skills](https://github.com/ChandlerWeeks/chandler-skills/tree/HEAD/principle-divide-the-work))
- **principle-do-not-block-the-human**: Apply when tempted to ask 'should I do X?' on reversible work. Proceed, present the result, let the human course-correct after the fact; reserve confirmation for irreversible actions. ([fabiommendes/codehood](https://github.com/fabiommendes/codehood/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/principle-do-not-block-the-human) · 3 copies)
- **principle-do-not-block-the-human**: Aplique quando tiver a tentação de perguntar 'devo fazer X?' em trabalho reversível. Prossiga, apresente o resultado e deixe o humano corrigir o rumo depois; reserve a confirmação para ações irreversíveis. ([fabiommendes/skills](https://github.com/fabiommendes/skills/tree/HEAD/pt-BR/principles/principle-do-not-block-the-human))
- **principle-documentation**: ドキュメントに何を載せ、何を載せないかの原則と事例。リポジトリのドキュメント、エージェント向けの指示ファイル、コードコメントを書くとき、見直すときに使う。 ([sasamuku/dotfiles](https://github.com/sasamuku/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-documentation) · ★10)
- **principle-documentation-and-adrs**: Records architecture decisions (ADRs) and the docs around a change. Use when making a decision that's expensive to reverse, changing a public API, shipping user-facing behaviour, or updating a README, changelog, or rules file. ([joseiciano/dotfiles](https://github.com/joseiciano/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/ai-clis/global-ai/output/claude/skills/principle-documentation-and-adrs) · 2 copies)
- **principle-dont-invent-problems**: Applies when a whats-going-on stage decides whether a concern is a problem, whether to classify it, or how sure to be. Do not invent a problem the input does not support. ([unfriction-ai/whats-going-on](https://github.com/unfriction-ai/whats-going-on/tree/HEAD/plugins/whats-going-on/skills/whats-going-on/principle-dont-invent-problems) · 2 copies)
- **principle-dont-solve-yet**: Applies when a whats-going-on stage is about to diagnose, brainstorm, or recommend. Stop once the problem is identified. ([unfriction-ai/whats-going-on](https://github.com/unfriction-ai/whats-going-on/tree/HEAD/plugins/whats-going-on/skills/whats-going-on/principle-dont-solve-yet) · 2 copies)
- **principle-doubt-driven-development**: Subjects non-trivial decisions to a fresh-context adversarial review before they stand. Use before an architectural decision under uncertainty or a non-trivial commit, when asserting a property you can't prove (safe, idempotent, scales, matches the spec), when working in unfamiliar code, or when the stakes are high or irreversible (auth, security, migrations, deploys). ([joseiciano/dotfiles](https://github.com/joseiciano/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/ai-clis/global-ai/output/claude/skills/principle-doubt-driven-development) · 2 copies)
- **principle-dry**: 识别并消除知识重复，确保系统中每条知识有唯一权威表示。当需要重构代码、整理文档、优化配置、建立单一数据源时使用。覆盖代码重复、数据冗余、文档重复三大维度，提供具体消除策略和权衡建议。 ([tikazyq/agentic-spec-forge](https://github.com/tikazyq/agentic-spec-forge/tree/HEAD/AGENTIC_SPEC_FORGE/spec_stage_skill/common_normal/principle-dry) · ★5)
- **principle-dry**: One home for every piece of knowledge. ([Nusnus/nustack](https://github.com/Nusnus/nustack/tree/HEAD/plugin/skills/principle-dry))
- **principle-durable-loop**: Apply when the outcome should continue with no person feeding the next task. Build the access first. ([jl-cmd-projects/DeepStack](https://github.com/jl-cmd-projects/DeepStack/tree/HEAD/plugins/deepstack/skills/principle-durable-loop))
- **principle-earn-every-element**: WHEN adding UI controls, settings, options, API parameters or document sections; NOT observing a user journey or weighing scope trade-offs; keeps only elements with a stated user need. ([mintuz/skills](https://github.com/mintuz/skills/tree/HEAD/src/principle/skills/principle-earn-every-element) · ★37)
- **principle-encode-corrections**: Make recurring corrections durable in tests, checks, scripts, or structure. ([leehosanganson/dotfiles](https://github.com/leehosanganson/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/ai/.config/ai/skills/principle-encode-corrections))
- **principle-encode-lessons-in-structure**: Apply when you catch yourself writing the same instruction a second time, or notice a recurring correction. Encode the rule as a lint, metadata flag, runtime check, or script instead of more text. ([storybookjs/storybook](https://github.com/storybookjs/storybook/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-encode-lessons-in-structure) · ★92k · 10k installs · 994 copies)

- **principle-encode-lessons-in-structure**: Apply when you catch yourself writing the same instruction a second time, or notice a recurring correction. - **principle-encode-lessons-in-structure**: Use when you catch yourself repeating an instruction or the user corrects the same thing again. Convert the lesson into a check that breaks on violation (a lint rule, type, runtime assertion, or script) in place of more prose. ([hashgraph-online/awesome-codex-plugins](https://github.com/hashgraph-online/awesome-codex-plugins/tree/HEAD/plugins/requie/endostack/skills/principle-encode-lessons-in-structure) · ★1.6k · 5 copies)
- **principle-encode-lessons-in-structure**: Turn recurring guidance into a type, lint, test, metadata flag, generator, runtime check, or skill so the system carries the lesson. Use when when the same corrective instruction appears more than once. ([sreichholf/dreamDroid](https://github.com/sreichholf/dreamDroid/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-encode-lessons-in-structure) · ★117 · 3 copies)
- **principle-encode-lessons-in-structure**: Apply when you catch yourself writing the same instruction a second time, or notice a recurring correction. Encode the rule as a lint, metadata flag, runtime check, or script instead of more text. Observe required inputs first; HOLD when they are missing rather than guessing. ([jeremybrasher/grokbot-skills](https://github.com/jeremybrasher/grokbot-skills/tree/HEAD/collections/pstack/skills/principle-encode-lessons-in-structure) · ★38)
- **principle-encode-lessons-in-structure**: WHEN a verified lesson recurs, the same failure or correction having happened at least twice; NOT for an unverified hypothesis, a single occurrence or a full recurrence investigation; chooses one narrow structural guard, or one instruction for a judgement-only lesson. ([mintuz/skills](https://github.com/mintuz/skills/tree/HEAD/src/principle/skills/principle-encode-lessons-in-structure) · ★37)
- **principle-encode-lessons-in-structure**: Apply when you catch yourself repeating a rule or fixing the same class of mistake twice. Encode it as a lint, schema check, ratchet, or script instead of more prose. - **principle-encode-over-document**: When a documented rule has been violated twice, stop strengthening the documentation and encode the rule as a type, helper, or tool-checked structure. Use when deciding where a new rule should live, when a review finds a repeat regression, or when a checklist keeps growing instead of getting sharper. ([oskarc/AssetHub](https://github.com/oskarc/AssetHub/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/principle-encode-over-document) · ★2 · 2 copies)
- **principle-encode-the-procedure**: When you do a project procedure a second time, or the user corrects how you did it, propose or update a project skill. ([kpeez/agent-toolbox](https://github.com/kpeez/agent-toolbox/tree/HEAD/plugins/lab/skills/principle-encode-the-procedure))
- **principle-enforce-access-explicitly**: Apply to any data read or write path. CRUD, field-level security, and sharing are deliberate and stated: with sharing on the class, WITH USER_MODE or Security.stripInaccessible() on the data path, and a written reason for every without sharing or system-mode exception. ([krika2810/sfstack](https://github.com/krika2810/sfstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-enforce-access-explicitly) · ★1)
- **principle-enforce-distributed-authority**: Prevent stale or competing workers from committing protected changes through atomic claims, conditional writes, and fencing. Mandatory when work can move between workers, leases expire, leadership changes, or failover can leave an earlier actor running. ([ElmoJones3/opinionated](https://github.com/ElmoJones3/opinionated/tree/HEAD/skills/systems/principle-enforce-distributed-authority) · ★1)
- **principle-enforce-idempotent-effects**: Make equivalent requests converge on one logical effect through receiver-side identity and atomic arbitration. Mandatory when a command or external side effect may be delivered or invoked more than once. ([ElmoJones3/opinionated](https://github.com/ElmoJones3/opinionated/tree/HEAD/skills/systems/principle-enforce-idempotent-effects) · ★1)
- **principle-enforce-where-it-is-stored**: Apply when more than one client (a web app, an API caller, an agent or MCP tool, an import job) can write the same data, or when a client fills in or corrects a field before sending it. The system that stores the data enforces its rules for every writer; a client that covers for a missing server rule hides the gap. ([quotegoat-ux/bleat](https://github.com/quotegoat-ux/bleat/tree/HEAD/plugins/bleat/skills/principle-enforce-where-it-is-stored))
- **principle-engagement-is-not-consent**: Apply when a signal such as a like, comment, follow or visit is used as a reason to reach out or to keep data. ([ycanerden/gluely](https://github.com/ycanerden/gluely/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-engagement-is-not-consent) · ★3)
- **principle-engineer-tdd**: Strictly enforce outside-in TDD on every implementation task. Acceptance test first from the issue's EARS/Gherkin scenarios; modules grown via one-behavior RED → GREEN → REFACTOR loops with fake adapters at seams; real adapters earn contract tests; wiring proved by the acceptance test going green. Each step is its own commit per the caller's commit-message template. Encodes scaffold-vs-production boundaries, mandatory edge-case coverage, banned test anti-patterns, and the iron rules. ([MartinKChen/harness-claude-code](https://github.com/MartinKChen/harness-claude-code/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-engineer-tdd))
- **principle-error-design**: Apply when adding or changing an error: its type, message, public shape, status code or retry guidance. Say what failed and why, end on a way forward, carry typed context, keep reassurance provable, and translate each failure deliberately at the boundary. ([YuukiFST/agent-dotfiles](https://github.com/YuukiFST/agent-dotfiles/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-error-design))
- **principle-error-handling**: Error handling principles — errors as values vs exceptions, wrapping and context chains, retry with exponential backoff and jitter, circuit breakers and bulkheads, log-once boundary discipline. Auto-load when discussing errors as values, retry, exponential backoff, jitter, fail fast, fail soft, idempotent retry, error wrapping, or deadlines. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-error-handling) · ★3)
- **principle-event-driven**: Event-driven architecture — event sourcing, CQRS, sagas, schema evolution, consumer groups, partitions, idempotent handlers, outbox pattern, dead letter queues. Auto-load when designing event-driven systems, evaluating event sourcing or CQRS, planning saga workflows, implementing idempotent consumers or the outbox pattern, managing dead letter queues, or assessing whether event-driven architecture fits the problem. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-event-driven) · ★3)
- **principle-evidence**: 技术信号、笔记经验或模型判断不一致，或者需要提供引用时使用。核对代码事实与实际读取的原文，避免把推断写成信号。 ([aicoder2048/pi-super-agent](https://github.com/aicoder2048/pi-super-agent/tree/HEAD/docs/modules/examples/stock-ta/skills/principle-evidence))
- **principle-evidence-before-verdicts**: Apply when investigating, reviewing, or reporting conclusions. Support verdicts with inspected evidence and distinguish defects from uncertainty and preferences. ([lexedwards/daedalus](https://github.com/lexedwards/daedalus/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-evidence-before-verdicts))
- **principle-evidence-in-every-claim**: Apply to every report, reply, PR body, and review. Each claim carries its evidence or its label (measured, inferred, or guess) in the same sentence. Never report a check you did not run, and never link something you did not open. ([Nikshay1/nystack](https://github.com/Nikshay1/nystack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-evidence-in-every-claim))
- **principle-evidence-not-claims**: Apply before you report any test status, coverage, fix, or readiness. A verdict must rest on an executed run against the artifact under test, quoted and rerunnable. Proxies such as collect-only, compiling, a dry run, or green CI on another commit are NOT RUN. ([Jacky97s/qstack](https://github.com/Jacky97s/qstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-evidence-not-claims) · ★1)
- **principle-evidence-over-report**: When a report and the repository disagree — a worker claims success with no commit, a tracker says done but the gate is red — the repo is right and the report is narrative. Apply whenever consuming status from tickets, workers, comments, dashboards, or pipeline state. ([vraj-ai/skills](https://github.com/vraj-ai/skills/tree/HEAD/principles/principle-evidence-over-report) · ★3)
- **principle-evolve-and-restore-state**: Keep durable data, messages, work, authority, and external effects safe across mixed-version deployment, rollback, migration, failover, backup, and restore. Mandatory when changing a durable representation or protocol, running mixed versions, resuming old work, or designing rollback, migration, backup, restore, or failover. ([ElmoJones3/opinionated](https://github.com/ElmoJones3/opinionated/tree/HEAD/skills/systems/principle-evolve-and-restore-state) · ★1)
- **principle-excuse-first**: Apply before writing any outreach. Every message needs an honest, recent, relevant reason to exist. ([ycanerden/gluely](https://github.com/ycanerden/gluely/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-excuse-first) · ★3)
- **principle-exhaust-design-space**: Apply when a novel interaction or architecture decision has no useful precedent. Build two or three competing prototypes and compare them before committing. ([ImAno177/cstack](https://github.com/ImAno177/cstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-exhaust-design-space))
- **principle-exhaust-the-design-space**: Apply when facing a novel UI interaction or architectural decision with no precedent in the codebase. Build 2-3 competing prototypes and compare side by side before committing. ([elizaOS/eliza](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-exhaust-the-design-space) · ★20k · 10k installs · 946 copies)

([hashgraph-online/awesome-codex-plugins](https://github.com/hashgraph-online/awesome-codex-plugins/tree/HEAD/plugins/requie/endostack/skills/principle-exhaust-the-design-space) · ★1.6k · 5 copies)
- **principle-exhaust-the-design-space**: Build a few materially different cheap prototypes and compare evidence before committing to one design. Use when for novel interactions or architecture with no strong precedent. ([sreichholf/dreamDroid](https://github.com/sreichholf/dreamDroid/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-exhaust-the-design-space) · ★117 · 3 copies)
- **principle-exhaust-the-design-space**: Apply when facing a novel UI interaction or architectural decision with no precedent in the codebase. Build 2-3 competing prototypes and compare side by side before committing. Observe required inputs first; HOLD when they are missing rather than guessing. ([jeremybrasher/grokbot-skills](https://github.com/jeremybrasher/grokbot-skills/tree/HEAD/collections/pstack/skills/principle-exhaust-the-design-space) · ★38)
- **principle-exhaust-the-design-space**: WHEN a novel interaction or architecture choice has material uncertainty; NOT for settled mechanical changes; compares a few distinct cheap alternatives before commitment. ([mintuz/skills](https://github.com/mintuz/skills/tree/HEAD/src/principle/skills/principle-exhaust-the-design-space) · ★37)
- **principle-exhaust-the-design-space**: Gated principle: apply on net-new UI surfaces, material layout redesigns, greenfield architecture, or when explicitly requested — never on routine bugfix tickets. Diverge into 2-3 options and autonomously commit the winner. ([watt-mind/factory](https://github.com/watt-mind/factory/tree/HEAD/dist/codex/skills/principle-exhaust-the-design-space) · ★20 · 7 copies)

([rimoir/ref-agent-plugins-ko](https://github.com/rimoir/ref-agent-plugins-ko/tree/HEAD/pstack/skills/principle-explain-the-number))
- **principle-explain-the-number**: Apply before you measure, and before you trust, report, or act on a number you measured: a speedup, a regression, a throughput, a latency, or an eval result. Find what limits it, and rule out that it measured something other than the work you think. Explicitly invoke this pstack entry as $principle-explain-the-number. ([starrybamboo/agent-skills](https://github.com/starrybamboo/agent-skills/tree/HEAD/harness/codex/skills/principle-explain-the-number))
- **principle-explain-the-number**: Apply before measuring, and before trusting, reporting, or acting on measured results such as speedups, regressions, throughput, latency, or evaluation scores. ([taekwondodev/skills](https://github.com/taekwondodev/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-explain-the-number))
- **principle-explain-the-number**: Apply before you measure, and before you trust, report, or act on a number you measured: a speedup, a regression, a throughput, a latency, a count, or an eval result. Find what limits it, and rule out that it measured something other than the work you think. ([YuukiFST/agent-dotfiles](https://github.com/YuukiFST/agent-dotfiles/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-explain-the-number))
- **principle-explain-the-number**: 测量之前使用，也在你信任、报告或依据一个测到的数字行动之前使用：加速、回退、吞吐、延迟，或一次 eval 结果。找出限制它的东西，并排除它量到的不是你以为的那项工作。 ([zheyi420/Dev-Wiki](https://github.com/zheyi420/Dev-Wiki/tree/HEAD/AI/Agent/plugins/pstack/skills/principle-explain-the-number))
- **principle-explain-the-number-pi**: Pi adaptation: Apply before you measure, and before you trust, report, or act on a number you measured: a speedup, a regression, a throughput, a latency, or an eval result. Find what limits it, and rule out that it measured something other than the work you think. ([evoclock/pi-poteto-mode](https://github.com/evoclock/pi-poteto-mode/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-explain-the-number-pi) · ★9)
- **principle-explicit-errors**: Apply whenever code handles an exception (catch, suppress, translate, retry), decides to skip, drop, truncate, or stop early on data (an empty parse, a missing lookup, an unpriced row, a lookback or coverage cap, a loop that reads only the first source), or writes the text of an error, failure, or rejection message that a person or agent will read to fix the problem. ([EdbertChan/catstack](https://github.com/EdbertChan/catstack/tree/HEAD/corpus/skills/principle-explicit-errors) · ★2)
- **principle-exploratory-vs-confirmatory**: Apply when planning or reporting an analysis. Pre-state hypotheses and the analysis plan, and label anything found after looking as post hoc. ([taixuann/rstack](https://github.com/taixuann/rstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-exploratory-vs-confirmatory) · ★1)
- **principle-extend-at-evidenced-seams**: Apply when you add a required variant or decide whether code needs an extension point. Base the choice on known requirements or a concrete testing need. ([nilpath/nilpath-marketplace](https://github.com/nilpath/nilpath-marketplace/tree/HEAD/dist/claude/plugins/nstack/skills/principle-extend-at-evidenced-seams) · 3 copies)
- **principle-extract**: 从已完成的决策中提炼可复用原则，写入原则库。 当用户拍板后、决策分析结束、或用户说"这个经验值得记住"时触发。 ([picsky/flowos](https://github.com/picsky/flowos/tree/HEAD/templates/skills/principle-extract))
- **principle-facts-over-judgment**: Apply when writing any analysis, summary, or recommendation. Keep facts, assumptions, judgments, and predictions on separate lines, each labeled. ([frinfo702/cowork](https://github.com/frinfo702/cowork/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-facts-over-judgment))
- **principle-fail-fast**: Code that fails where a wrong state is first detected, with an error that says how to recover, and holds an expected absence in its type. ([Nusnus/nustack](https://github.com/Nusnus/nustack/tree/HEAD/plugin/skills/principle-fail-fast))
- **principle-fail-loud**: 例外を握りつぶす、既定値で埋める、フォールバックで続ける、「たぶん大丈夫」で進めそうなときに適用する。見過ごしを許さず、エラーを出して止める。 ([B16B1RD/bb-writ](https://github.com/B16B1RD/bb-writ/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-fail-loud))
- **principle-failing-test-first**: Apply before writing production code. Write the failing check first, at the fastest level that expresses the behavior, and watch it fail for the right reason. ([daryl-tg/deez-skills](https://github.com/daryl-tg/deez-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-failing-test-first))
- **principle-falsify-or-slogan**: Apply when accepting a nominated constraint. Require what would prove it wrong within a fixed window (metric, queue, cycle time, cash, conversion, blocked dependency). ([Shixuuu/grokbot-pi-templates](https://github.com/Shixuuu/grokbot-pi-templates/tree/HEAD/templates/community/research-briefings/first-principles-t5qkap/skills/principle-falsify-or-slogan) · ★6)
- **principle-fast-tests**: Apply when writing, reviewing or speeding up slow tests. A test runs under 1 second and a service's suite under 5 minutes, and speed comes from the test's shape, never from lower fidelity. ([kapetan-io/dispatch](https://github.com/kapetan-io/dispatch/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-fast-tests))
- **principle-feature-branch-isolation**: Apply before starting any change. Work happens on daryl/&lt;kebab-words&gt; in its own worktree, never on main, never in the primary worktree. ([daryl-tg/deez-skills](https://github.com/daryl-tg/deez-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-feature-branch-isolation))
- **principle-fewest-moving-parts**: Apply when choosing a design or an implementation. Pick the least fancy, simplest, most robust option with the least logic that still scales. ([Torus403/skills](https://github.com/Torus403/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-fewest-moving-parts))
- **principle-finish-or-report**: Apply at the end of any run. Never deliver a partial silently. Either the whole thing is done, or say precisely what is left and why. ([daryl-tg/deez-skills](https://github.com/daryl-tg/deez-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-finish-or-report))
- **principle-finish-the-migration**: Apply to internal migrations without concrete external or persisted compatibility requirements. Migrate every in-scope caller and remove the old contract. ([lachieh/skills](https://github.com/lachieh/skills/tree/HEAD/.apm/skills/principle-finish-the-migration) · 2 copies)
- **principle-fix-root-cause**: Use when something is broken and the cause is unknown ([sansernonline/SQT-Marketplace](https://github.com/sansernonline/SQT-Marketplace/tree/HEAD/dist/codex/e-commerce/.agents/skills/principle-fix-root-cause) · 25 copies)
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: Apply when debugging. Trace each symptom back to the first place the system went wrong and fix it there. A guard is a fix only when the state it handles is legitimate. - **principle-fix-root-causes**: Apply when debugging. Trace each symptom to its root cause and fix it there; reproduce first, ask why until you reach it, resist nil-check guards that silence crashes. ([nightly-labs/openbot](https://github.com/nightly-labs/openbot/tree/HEAD/marketplace/production-catalog/bundles/pstack/skills/principle-fix-root-causes) · ★9.5k · 403 copies)
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: Apply when debugging. Trace each symptom back to the first place the system went wrong and fix it there. A guard is a fix only when the state it handles is legitimate. ([backnotprop/pstack](https://github.com/backnotprop/pstack) · ★4.5k · 11k installs)
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: Apply when debugging. Trace each symptom back to the first place the system went wrong and fix it there. A guard is a fix only when the state it handles is legitimate. ([michael-denyer/pstack-claude](https://github.com/michael-denyer/pstack-claude) · ★4.5k · 1.1k installs)
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: Use for any debugging or bug fix. Repair the earliest point where the system decided wrongly, rather than where the bad state showed up. A guard is a real fix only for a state the system is permitted to reach. ([hashgraph-online/awesome-codex-plugins](https://github.com/hashgraph-online/awesome-codex-plugins/tree/HEAD/plugins/requie/endostack/skills/principle-fix-root-causes) · ★1.6k · 5 copies)
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: Reproduce the symptom, trace causality until one mechanism explains it, and fix that mechanism rather than compensating for downstream effects. Use when during debugging and incident repair. ([sreichholf/dreamDroid](https://github.com/sreichholf/dreamDroid/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-fix-root-causes) · ★117 · 3 copies)
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: Apply when debugging. Trace each symptom to its root cause and fix it there; reproduce first, ask why until you reach it, resist nil-check guards that silence crashes. Observe required inputs first; HOLD when they are missing rather than guessing. ([jeremybrasher/grokbot-skills](https://github.com/jeremybrasher/grokbot-skills/tree/HEAD/collections/pstack/skills/principle-fix-root-causes) · ★38)
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: WHEN debugging a reproducible defect or distinguishing competing causes; NOT for removing legitimate guards or widening authorized scope; repairs the evidenced cause and rechecks the failure. ([mintuz/skills](https://github.com/mintuz/skills/tree/HEAD/src/principle/skills/principle-fix-root-causes) · ★37)
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: Apply when debugging any failure or test flake. Trace each symptom back to the first place the system went wrong and fix it there; a guard is a fix only when the state it handles is legitimate. ([watt-mind/factory](https://github.com/watt-mind/factory/tree/HEAD/dist/codex/skills/principle-fix-root-causes) · ★20 · 7 copies)
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: pstack, typed-only. Load only when the user names this skill or a loaded pstack skill routes to it. Apply when debugging. Trace each symptom back to the first place the system went wrong and fix it there. A guard is a fix only when the state it handles is legitimate. - **principle-fix-root-causes**: Fix root causes. Apply when debugging a failure or repairing a finding. ([erickb336/sage-stack](https://github.com/erickb336/sage-stack/tree/HEAD/plugins/sage-codex/skills/principle-fix-root-causes) · ★2 · 2 copies)
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: Apply when debugging. Trace the first wrong decision or missing handling of a legitimate state, and fix it there; do not hide invalid states at the symptom. ([fullerzz/agent-skills](https://github.com/fullerzz/agent-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-fix-root-causes) · ★2)
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: Apply when debugging. Trace each symptom back to the first place the system went wrong and fix it there. A guard is a fix only when the state it handles is legitimate. Use for /principle-fix-root-causes. ([lovrozagar/grunt](https://github.com/lovrozagar/grunt/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-fix-root-causes) · ★2 · 20 copies)
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: Apply when debugging or fixing a bug. Trace the symptom back to the first place the system did something wrong and fix it there. A guard, retry or catch is a fix only when the state it handles is legitimate. ([patryksztuczka/boberstack](https://github.com/patryksztuczka/boberstack/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-fix-root-causes) · ★2)
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: Use when debugging an error, crash, failing or flaky test, or wrong output ("debug this", "fix this error", "it broke after..."), and before adding a guard, retry, or catch around a symptom. - **principle-fix-root-causes**: 디버깅할 때 적용한다. 각 증상을 시스템이 처음 잘못된 곳까지 추적해 거기서 고친다. 방어 코드는 그것이 다루는 상태가 정당할 때만 수정이다. ([yjb94/jbstack](https://github.com/yjb94/jbstack/tree/HEAD/core/skills/principle-fix-root-causes) · ★2)
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: Apply when debugging or fixing a bug: fix the first place the system went wrong, not where the symptom surfaced, and every place with the same fault. ([cevr/dotfiles](https://github.com/cevr/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-fix-root-causes) · ★1)
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: 不具合を再現し、症状を隠す処理より根本原因を直す。 ([dooochi8/dev-base](https://github.com/dooochi8/dev-base/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/principle-fix-root-causes) · ★1 · 2 copies)
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: Use when fixing a bug or a test that fails unexpectedly: reproduce it first, trace the symptom to its cause, and fix the cause rather than the place the symptom appeared. ([fabricio21x/fbs](https://github.com/fabricio21x/fbs/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-fix-root-causes) · ★1)
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: Apply when debugging a wrong number, a failing pipeline, a broken notebook, or a failing test. Trace each symptom back to the first step where the data or code went wrong, and fix it there. A guard is a fix only when the bad state is legitimate. ([hidenobunagai/astack](https://github.com/hidenobunagai/astack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-fix-root-causes) · ★1)
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: Apply when a workaround would hide a defect that can be reproduced and traced. ([jaydubya818/MissionControl](https://github.com/jaydubya818/MissionControl/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-fix-root-causes) · ★1 · 2 copies)
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: Apply when debugging. Trace each symptom to its root cause and fix it there: reproduce first, ask why until you reach the mechanism, no guards that silence crashes, fix the pattern not the instance. ([krika2810/sfstack](https://github.com/krika2810/sfstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-fix-root-causes) · ★1)
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: Apply when debugging a bug, crash, flaky test, or failure after restart, or when tempted to add a nil check, retry, or try/catch to make an error go away. Reproduce first, ask why until you reach the root cause, fix it there, and fix every instance of the pattern. - **principle-fix-root-causes**: Apply when fixing a bug, regression, or recurring operational problem. Find the condition that produces the failure and remove it instead of adding a local suppression. ([MattiaIppoliti/tia-skills](https://github.com/MattiaIppoliti/tia-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/engineering/principle-fix-root-causes) · ★1)
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: Fixes wrong behaviour where it first went wrong (data model, schema, algorithm, prompt) instead of patching the symptom, lets failures surface as errors, and fixes the whole class, never one instance. Use when fixing a bug, failing test, error or wrong model answer, before adding a guard, fallback, default, retry or special case. - **principle-fix-root-causes**: Nội quy khi debug: lần mỗi triệu chứng về chỗ đầu tiên hệ thống đi sai và sửa ở đó. Guard chỉ tính là bản sửa khi trạng thái nó xử lý là hợp lệ. ([lichihouse/claude-plugins](https://github.com/lichihouse/claude-plugins/tree/HEAD/plugins/pstack/skills/principle-fix-root-causes))
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: Only use when poteto-mode is active for this task. Apply when debugging. Trace each symptom back to the first place the system went wrong and fix it there. A guard is a fix only when the state it handles is legitimate. ([mallochio/pstack-ports](https://github.com/mallochio/pstack-ports/tree/HEAD/opencode/skills/principle-fix-root-causes) · 2 copies)
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: Apply when debugging, when a leftover process, file or lock blocks the next step, when repointing a hardcoded path, host or constant, when fixing a review finding, or when writing up an incident. Trace each symptom back to the first place the system went wrong and fix it there. A guard is a fix only when the state it handles is legitimate. ([MaxFabian25/pstack-harness-adaptations](https://github.com/MaxFabian25/pstack-harness-adaptations/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-fix-root-causes))
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: Traces each symptom to the first place the system went wrong and fixes it there. ([NikitaKurpas/agents](https://github.com/NikitaKurpas/agents/tree/HEAD/plugins/standards/skills/principle-fix-root-causes))
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: Apply while debugging. Trace each symptom back to the first place the system went wrong and fix it there. A guard or fallback is a fix only when the state it handles is legitimate. ([Nikshay1/nystack](https://github.com/Nikshay1/nystack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-fix-root-causes))
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: Use when debugging a symptom: reproduce it, trace it to the root cause, fix it there and in same-pattern instances, and reject silencing guards. After restarts, suspect stale state first. ([PatrickFanella/patstack](https://github.com/PatrickFanella/patstack/tree/HEAD/library/skills/principle-fix-root-causes) · 2 copies)
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: 适用于调试时。把每个症状追溯到系统第一次出错的地方，在那里修。加一层防护只有在它处理的状态本来就该存在时才算修复。 ([Praying/pstack-zh](https://github.com/Praying/pstack-zh/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-fix-root-causes))
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: 디버깅할 때 적용한다. 증상마다 시스템이 처음 잘못된 곳까지 거슬러 올라가 거기서 고친다. 가드는 그것이 다루는 상태가 정당할 때만 수정이다. ([rimoir/ref-agent-plugins-ko](https://github.com/rimoir/ref-agent-plugins-ko/tree/HEAD/pstack/skills/principle-fix-root-causes))
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: デバッグ時に適用する。各症状を根本原因までたどり、そこで修正する。まず再現し、根本原因に達するまで「なぜ」を問い、クラッシュを黙らせる nil チェックのガードに抵抗する。 ([seiei-sogen/agent-skills](https://github.com/seiei-sogen/agent-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/pstack-skills/principle-fix-root-causes))
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: For the Box kitchen project only. Apply when debugging. Trace each symptom to its root cause and fix it there; reproduce first, ask why until you reach it, resist nil-check guards that silence crashes. ([shouteck/omakase-box](https://github.com/shouteck/omakase-box/tree/HEAD/.devin/plugins/box-pstack/skills/principle-fix-root-causes))
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: Apply when debugging a failing test, bug, or unexpected behavior (not trivial typos). Trace each symptom back to the first place the system went wrong and fix it there. A guard is a fix only when the state it handles is legitimate. ([snooptheone/desu-ai-plugins](https://github.com/snooptheone/desu-ai-plugins/tree/HEAD/plugins/desustack/skills/principle-fix-root-causes))
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: Apply when debugging. Trace each symptom back to the first place the system went wrong and fix it there. A guard is a fix only when the state it handles is legitimate. Explicitly invoke this pstack entry as $principle-fix-root-causes. ([starrybamboo/agent-skills](https://github.com/starrybamboo/agent-skills/tree/HEAD/harness/codex/skills/principle-fix-root-causes))
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: Apply when debugging any failure, or when a fix looks like a guard around a crash rather than a change to the cause. Reproduce first, ask why until you reach the root cause, and fix it there instead of adding a check that only silences the symptom. ([yestheboxer/jig](https://github.com/yestheboxer/jig/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-fix-root-causes))
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: Apply when debugging a bug, test failure, crash, or unexpected behavior. Reproduce first, trace the symptom back to the first place the system went wrong, and fix it there. A guard is a fix only when the state it handles is legitimate. ([YuukiFST/agent-dotfiles](https://github.com/YuukiFST/agent-dotfiles/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-fix-root-causes))
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: 调试时使用。把每个症状追回到系统第一次走错的地方，并在那里修好。守卫只有在它处理的状态是合法的时候才算修复。 ([zheyi420/Dev-Wiki](https://github.com/zheyi420/Dev-Wiki/tree/HEAD/AI/Agent/plugins/pstack/skills/principle-fix-root-causes))
- **principle-fix-root-causes-pi**: Pi adaptation: Apply when debugging. Trace each symptom back to the first place the system went wrong and fix it there. A guard is a fix only when the state it handles is legitimate. ([evoclock/pi-poteto-mode](https://github.com/evoclock/pi-poteto-mode/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-fix-root-causes-pi) · ★9)
- **principle-fix-the-bar-first**: WHEN comparing, racing, iterating on or reviewing candidates, existing or new; NOT for a gate that has already failed or for choosing which checks to run; records the selection criteria before evaluation and holds them after results. ([mintuz/skills](https://github.com/mintuz/skills/tree/HEAD/src/principle/skills/principle-fix-the-bar-first) · ★37)
- **principle-fix-the-latent-issue**: Apply when a request or visible defect may be a downstream symptom. Choose the right problem from the user goal, then fix its causal mechanism. ([lachieh/skills](https://github.com/lachieh/skills/tree/HEAD/.apm/skills/principle-fix-the-latent-issue) · 2 copies)
- **principle-fixes-need-a-yes**: Apply when you see a mechanical error in the user's draft: citation format, unit format, a typo. List it; change the file only after the user accepts that item. ([taixuann/rstack](https://github.com/taixuann/rstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-fixes-need-a-yes) · ★1)
- **principle-flag-your-own-corrections**: Apply whenever you are about to admit fault in any wording — a fact, number, claim, or check you already stated turns out to be wrong, vacuous, premature, or unverified. Say so explicitly rather than quietly citing the corrected value, and treat the admission itself as a reflect trigger. Auto-fires because no regex can enumerate the ways a model admits error. ([EdbertChan/catstack](https://github.com/EdbertChan/catstack/tree/HEAD/corpus/skills/principle-flag-your-own-corrections) · ★2)
- **principle-flaky-is-a-bug**: Apply to any intermittent result: a test that passes and fails on the same code, a retry that changed red to green, or a suite that fails only in CI. Find the source of the nondeterminism. Retries and longer timeouts hide it. ([Jacky97s/qstack](https://github.com/Jacky97s/qstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-flaky-is-a-bug) · ★1)
- **principle-fluid-interfaces**: Make gesture-driven and interactive UI feel physical: direct manipulation, interruptibility, springs, velocity handoff, momentum, rubber-banding, and spatial consistency. Use when building or reviewing drag, swipe, sheet, or any touch interaction. ([NickDabizaz/skills](https://github.com/NickDabizaz/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-fluid-interfaces))
- **principle-follow-official-docs**: Apply when the project uses a third-party library, platform, or service. Follow one option its current official docs prescribe, and cite that page in the PR. ([anas-yousef/pstack-grok](https://github.com/anas-yousef/pstack-grok/tree/HEAD/.cursor/skills/principle-follow-official-docs))
